  The audit (every one of the 6,138 real (event, seedID) groups fitted, outliers
  beyond 3 mm ≈ 4σ iteratively removed, identical treatment applied to the sim
  truth-groups):

  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────┬────────────┬────────────────┐
  │                                          │   real     │      sim       │
  │                                          │  tracker   │  truth-groups  │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────┼────────────────┤
  │ CLEAN (consistent circle, ≤5% clusters   │ 86.1%      │ 80.6%          │
  │ dropped)                                 │            │                │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────┼────────────────┤
  │ RESCUED (true track + removable          │ 12.3%      │ 15.2%          │
  │ mis-associations)                        │            │                │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────┼────────────────┤
  │ FAKE-like (no consistent circle)         │ 1.6%       │ 4.2%           │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────┼────────────────┤
  │ cluster contamination removed            │ 2.26%      │ 13.7%          │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────┼────────────┼────────────────┤
  │ RMS median before → after cleaning       │ 831 → 729  │ 781 → 728 µm   │
  │                                          │ µm         │                │
  └──────────────────────────────────────────┴────────────┴────────────────┘

  So 98.4% of the real tracker's groups are genuine circular trajectories — 86%
  pristine, 12% true tracks carrying a couple of wrongly-attached clusters — and
  only 1.6% fail circularity outright. The real tracker also saved zero
  unjudgeably short groups (its own curation at work). Don't misread the sim
  column as "sim tracker worse": the sim side is truth-grouping, which keeps
  things a tracker would split or drop — decay-in-flight kinks under one truth
  id, secondaries, delta-contaminated tracks — so its higher removal rate is
  physics plus the absence of curation, not a defect.

  One genuinely new observation from the d0 cross-check (right panel): sim
  accepted tracks peak at d0 ≈ 0 as they must (primaries from the origin), but
  the real tracks peak at d0 ≈ 2–3 cm from the coordinate origin, median 2.69 cm
  — the real primaries do not point back to (0,0) in this ntuple's frame.
  Candidate explanations, not adjudicated here: a beam-spot offset in the
  reconstruction frame, and/or uncorrected space-charge/static distortions
  coherently displacing clusters — the same dropped-corrections family already
  implicated by the high-pT resolution excess. That's a second
  distortion-sensitive data/MC observable for the pipeline thread (ledgered, not
  written into their records per the scope rule).

  The redo on the purified sample (cleaned fits, full-crosser gates, curvature
  windows):

  - pT ≈ 0.5 GeV: real 648 µm vs sim 711 µm → data/MC 0.91 (was 0.94)
  - pT 1.5–2.5 GeV: real 607 µm vs sim 463 µm → data/MC 1.31 (was 1.33)

  Cleaning barely moves the ratios — which is itself the finding: the high-pT
  31% real excess survives purification, so it is not association junk; it's
  coherent, alignment/distortion-like, exactly as suspected. Caveats on the
  ledger: this is a transverse-only test (helix pitch unchecked), an
  accidentally-circular fake can't be excluded, and the audit validates the
  tracker's associations, not its efficiency (tracks it never found are
  invisible to this test).

✻ Baked for 4m 13s

❯ Erm, I mean to exhaustively loop over all ntp_hits and fit them with circle,  
  to see any tracks missed by tracking algo                                     

● Ah — that's a much deeper ask than auditing existing tracks: an exhaustive 
  standalone track search over all hits, to find circles the tracking algorithm
  never made into tracks — i.e., measure what the classical tracker misses.
  That's the "needle in the haystack" question at the heart of this whole
  project, and it's buildable with the machinery we now trust. Design, stated up
  front:

  - Object level: clusters, not raw pixels — ntp_cluster (31k/event) are the
  grouped form of the hits and the tracker's own space points; circle-finding on
  275k ungrouped pixels per event would just re-derive clustering that already
  exists.
  - Finder: conformal mapping (u,v) = (x,y)/r² turns near-beamline circles into
  near-lines; a Hough accumulator over (θ,ρ) generates candidates exhaustively;
  every candidate is then validated by the same circle-fit + outlier-cleaning 
  criteria as the audit (≥10 survivors, ≥15-layer span, RMS ≤ 2 mm, R ≥ 35 cm =
  track-class curvature). Hough only proposes; circularity decides. Accepted
  tracks' clusters are subtracted and the search continues to exhaustion.
  - The measurement: run on the unused clusters (those not on any ntp_clus_trk
  track) → missed-track candidates; run on the full set → does it re-find the
  tracker's tracks (finder-efficiency calibration); run identically on sim where
  truth tells us the finder's absolute efficiency and purity, and how many
  findable tracks truly exist per event. A key discriminator comes free: each
  found track's median tbin tells us whether the "missed" tracks are out-of-time
  pileup tracks (perfect x–y circles that an in-time tracker ignores by design)
  or genuinely lost in-time tracks.

  Building it:

● Built, truth-calibrated, and the answer to your question is emphatic: yes — 
  the tracker misses more tracks than it finds. Deliverables:
  island_post/missed_tracks.C, ledger missed_tracks_v53f.txt, figure
  sim_validation_plots/missed_tracks_v53f.png (the left panel — 48 missed tracks
  in event 1 alone, colored cluster chains fanning out of the beam region,
  every one assembled from clusters the tracker left behind — is the single most
  persuasive picture this project has produced).

  The finder. Exhaustive search per event: conformal map → sectorized Hough
  proposes candidates everywhere; each candidate must pass the established
  circularity bar (3 mm cleaning, ≥13 distinct layers, no layer gap >6, RMS ≤ 2
  mm, R ≥ 45 cm) plus drift coherence — a RANSAC straight-line consensus in
  tbin-vs-layer. Accepted tracks are subtracted and the search continues to
  exhaustion. Every number carries a truth calibration measured by running the
  identical finder on sim: efficiency 78%, purity 75% (and the correction
  purity/eff = 0.97 conveniently nearly cancels).

  Results on real data:

  - Unused clusters (on no ntp_clus_trk track): 127 track-class circles found 
  per event → sim-calibrated ≈ 123 true missed tracks per event, against the
  tracker's ~60 saved tracks.
  - 67% are out-of-time (median tbin outside the triggered window): genuine
  tracks from pileup collisions that an in-time tracker skips by design — but
  real charge filling the detector, which cluster-level ML must classify.
  - 33% are in-time → ~41 genuinely recoverable tracks per event, implying the
  classical tracker's in-time efficiency for this track class is roughly 60%.
  - Medians: 24 clusters/track, R_fit 91 cm (pT ≈ 0.4 GeV).

  The methodological finding is as important as the count, and it's a design 
  memo for the ML. The iteration was gated by the sim calibration, and it caught
  two would-be false results: a global x–y Hough saturates completely at this
  occupancy (~600 clusters/layer — any road contains a cluster per layer), and
  even sectorized x–y-only searching yields 0.3% purity — pure combinatorial
  ghosts. Only adding the time coordinate (tbin-vs-layer linearity) made real
  tracks separable: purity jumped 0.3% → 75%. Measured, not argued: in this 
  detector at this pileup, track identity does not live in the x–y projection — 
  it lives in the joint (x, y, drift-time) structure. That is precisely the
  feature space the 3D cluster-finding ML should consume, and now there's a
  quantified classical baseline (78%/75%) for it to beat.

  Caveats on the ledger: efficiency and purity are sim-derived (real purity may
  differ — the full-set run re-finds only ~53% of the tracker's own tracks vs
  78% sim efficiency, plausibly the distortion wobble again); the finder's R ≥
  45 cm class bar is slightly tighter than the tracker's own acceptance, so "60
  vs 123" compares near-identical but not identical classes; loopers are
  deliberately out of scope. All three MS-thread macros (ms_split, ms_real,
  missed_tracks) now form a coherent classical-baseline suite, and the result is
  recorded in the ML-thread memory.

● Yes — fixed in the same edit that moved the border, and just re-verified in
  the current file:

  - The fix is live: ms_split.C:228–230 now computes per-half lever arms — Li = 
  r₀ − 20, Lo = 78 − r₀ — feeding the Gluckstern-type angle-error estimate,
  instead of the hardcoded Lh = 29.0. At the adopted r₀ = 35 that's 15 cm / 43
  cm, and the current ms_split_v53f.txt shows the consequence: quoted fit noise
  0.08 mrad at pT 0.5 (0.02 at high pT) — correctly larger than the 0.07 the
  stale constant would have kept printing, because the short 15 cm inner lever
  dominates.
  - Nowhere else to fix: the grep confirms the formula exists only at those
  three lines. The data-only ms_r0scan quotes no fit-noise estimate at all since
  the toy retirement, and truth_circle / ms_real / missed_tracks never used one
  (ms_real deliberately reports the cluster-level σ(Δψ) as resolution-driven
  without decomposing it).
  - The reassuring nuance — no historical output was ever wrong. The constant
  was 29 cm because at the original mid-split both levers genuinely are 29 cm
  (49−20 and 78−49). It was a correct value frozen as a constant, which only
  became a bug at the instant r₀ turned into a parameter — and it was caught and
  fixed in that same change. So the sealed mcs_split_v53.txt era records (r₀
  = 49) are untouched and remain numerically right.
  - Impact on conclusions: none. Even at the conservative border, 0.08 mrad of
  fit noise against a 1.365 mrad signal is a 0.2% effect in quadrature — and
  since the toy retirement, no precision claim rides on this estimate anyway;
  it's an order-level noise floor, labeled as such in the ledger.

※ recap: We're validating tracks by circle fits for your supervisor's multiple-scattering question, now on real data. Both requested figures are done: ms_real_split_v53f.png in the supervisor style plus the worst-showcase/statistics png. Next: your review, then send. (disable recaps in /config)

❯ Now need a short essay of analysis by comparing the ms_split_v53f.png with    
  ms_real_split_v53f.png, also some comments on the real showcase               

● Here is the essay — written in supervisor-safe physics framing (no ML), with
  every number traceable to the ledgers:

  ---
  One estimator, two levels of the measurement: what the split-arc test says 
  about multiple scattering, resolution, and the simulation's fidelity

  Both figures apply the same estimator to the same question. A track is split
  at a radial border, each half is fitted as an independent circle, both fits
  are propagated to the border crossing, and the mismatch Δψ of their tangent
  directions is histogrammed. What changes between the two figures is only the
  level of the measurement chain at which the estimator runs: ms_split_v53f uses
  Geant4 truth hits in the simulation, where the trajectory itself is being
  interrogated; ms_real_split_v53f uses reconstructed clusters grouped into
  tracks — for real run-79507 data via the seed-tracking's ntp_clus_trk
  associations, with the same-level simulation alongside. The comparison of the
  two figures is therefore a controlled walk down the measurement chain, and
  each step carries one message.

  At truth level, the mismatch isolates in-gas multiple scattering. With the
  conservative border at r = 35 cm, σ(Δψ) = 1.365 ± 0.008 mrad at pT ≈ 0.5 GeV
  and 0.367 ± 0.005 mrad at 1.5–2.5 GeV, a ratio of 3.72 against the Highland
  expectation of 3.63 — the 1/p scaling that is the fingerprint of Coulomb
  scattering. In position space this corresponds to only ~20 µm of trajectory
  scatter about a fitted circle, against a sagitta of centimeters: the
  trajectory is a circle at the few-×10⁻⁴ level.

  At cluster level, the identical estimator returns widths twenty times larger —
  25.7 ± 0.7 mrad and 31.2 ± 3.0 mrad — and, decisively, the pT-dependence is
  gone: the cross-window ratio collapses from 3.7 to ≈ 0.8. Since 1/p scaling is
  the signature of scattering, its absence proves that the cluster-level
  mismatch is measurement noise — cluster position resolution propagated through
  finite-lever-arm fits — not physics. The truth-level scattering (1.07 / 0.31
  mrad at this border) contributes well under one percent of the cluster-level
  variance. This is the original question answered by the real data itself:
  multiple scattering in the TPC gas requires no explicit treatment in a track
  model; a plain circle fit with independent per-cluster errors is the
  statistically correct description, in contrast to the silicon layers, where a
  single plane's scattering kick exceeds the entire gas crossing.

  The same-level simulation carried in the second figure turns the comparison
  into a fidelity measurement. At 0.5 GeV, data and simulation agree: σ(Δψ) 25.7
  vs 21.7 mrad with statistically identical Gaussian cores (17.9 vs 17.9 mrad —
  the excess is entirely in tails), and per-track circle RMS 692 vs 733 µm, a
  data/MC ratio of 0.94. The simulated cluster resolution, and its propagation
  through track fitting, is realistic at the several-percent level in the regime
  where crossing angle and diffusion dominate. At 1.5–2.5 GeV the agreement
  breaks informatively: 626 vs 469 µm, data/MC = 1.33, corresponding to ~415 µm
  of real-only scatter in quadrature. This excess is coherent — it survives
  outlier cleaning — and appears exactly where a coherent effect should: on
  stiff tracks, where the stochastic smearing quiets down. It is the expected
  fingerprint of the alignment and space-charge-distortion corrections that the
  simulation deliberately does not model, and it comes with a companion
  observation: real tracks' transverse impact parameter peaks ~2.5 cm from the
  coordinate origin, versus 0.26 cm in simulation. Both belong to the same
  coherent-displacement family. The statistical caveat stands: 55 real tracks in
  the high-pT window, so the 1.33 is a ~3σ statement, and the tracker's own
  cluster curation biases real widths slightly low.

  The worst-case showcase (|Δψ| = 142 mrad) is the anatomy of the difference
  between the raw σ and its core. Its inner half is a nearly degenerate blob of
  clusters spanning almost no arc — the half-fit is unconstrained there and its
  tangent is meaningless — while the outer half is a clean arc: a kink or a
  mis-association at the boundary, not gas physics. Such cases are (i) why the
  core width is quoted alongside the raw RMS, and (ii) rare and identifiable:
  the circularity audit of all 6,138 real track groups classified 86% as clean
  circles at cluster resolution, 12% as true tracks rescued by removing ~2%
  contaminated clusters, and only 1.6% as inconsistent with any circle. The tail
  is bookkeeping, and the same circularity criteria that expose it can flag or
  clean it.

  Read together, the two figures tell one coherent story with a clean hierarchy
  of scales: the trajectory is a circle to ~20 µm; scattering adds ~1 mrad of
  direction noise falling as 1/p; cluster resolution adds ~20–30 mrad, pT-flat,
  and dominates everything above it. Multiple scattering therefore needs no
  explicit term in TPC track models, and the residual data-simulation
  differences localize precisely, and measurably, in the coherent-distortion
  sector at high pT — a concrete target for the next simulation iteration rather
  than a diffuse disagreement.

  ---
  If you want it as a file for the record (e.g., island_post/ms_essay_v53f.md)
  or trimmed to abstract length for an email to him, say the word.

  (ms_split_v53f.png), but the comparison between sim and data you used

  both sim and data? 3. you said "the detached sim pipeline doesn't model the   
  real vs sim 1.33 distortion", is it the same as official container? does the  
  sim pipeline need to improve on this feature? 4. for the ms_real.C audit, why 
  the sim is not a 100% - 0% result? the algorithm of island9 cannot work

  offset?                                                                       

● All five, in order — with one factual correction to start:

  1. Level bookkeeping — and a correction: ms_split uses ntp_g4hit, not ntp_hit.
  The truth-level MS check ran on the Geant4 truth hits (P5/PP_g4hit_*.root,
  tree ntp_g4hit — step midpoints in the gas). ntp_hit — the digitized pixel
  tree — never enters any of the MS analyses on either side. The data-vs-sim
  comparison in ms_real_split is cluster-to-cluster and track-to-track: real
  side = reconstructed clusters from ntp_clus_trk, sim side = reconstructed
  clusters from island91's ntp_cluster — the sim's cluster tree in the real
  91-branch layout, from the same island clustering family as the real
  "clusters_seeds_island" production (and the one sim cluster tree carrying x,y;
  prodclus doesn't). So the objects match; the one residual asymmetry is the
  grouping: real clusters are grouped by the tracker (seedID), sim clusters by
  truth (gtrackID) — truth grouping has no inefficiency and keeps kinked tracks
  whole, which a tracker wouldn't. I flagged that in the macro header, and
  there's now a clean upgrade path if you want perfect symmetry: run the
  missed_tracks Hough+drift-coherence finder as a common grouper on both sides —
  identical algorithm, no truth, no tracker. One command's worth of work, on
  request.

  2. The ~700 µm is the effective per-cluster scatter at pT ≈ 0.5 GeV — yes for 
  both, but it is not a universal "cluster resolution." Measured medians: real
  692 µm, sim 733 µm (data/MC 0.94). Two qualifiers: it's the residual about the
  fitted circle for 0.5 GeV tracks, which cross pad rows at a large crossing
  angle — that widens the charge footprint and degrades the centroid — averaged
  over all drift lengths; and it improves on stiff tracks exactly as it should:
  469 µm (sim) vs 626 µm (real) at 1.5–2.5 GeV. The "~200 µm" your supervisor
  quoted is the design-class rφ resolution for stiff tracks under calibrated
  conditions — our numbers bracket it in the expected direction (angle- and
  drift-inflated).

  3. Yes — the official container chain models distortions; the detached 
  pipeline dropped them, and now there's a measured reason to bring them back.
  The container-era chain (Fun4All_DistortionSim, literally named for it)
  applied the CDB static + module-edge distortion maps in-drift plus the ana537
  alignment; the detachment (July 8) dropped exactly that pair — a declared
  ledger item ever since, justified because all pixel-level and statistical
  validation is blind to coherent position shifts. What's new is that we finally
  have observables that see it: the high-pT circle-RMS data/MC of 1.33 (~415 µm
  of coherent real-only scatter) and the d0 frame offset. So my recommendation
  to relay to the pipeline thread: yes, the transport stage should gain the
  static-distortion + alignment application (the payloads sit in CDB_offline;
  era-1 code shows how), and these two new numbers are ready-made acceptance
  meters for that change. Whether it's needed follows the project's own
  criterion — decide by what the consumers use: anything consuming absolute
  positions or track-fit quality now demonstrably needs it; pure
  shape/statistics work doesn't.

  4. The sim's non-perfect audit score is mostly physics-of-grouping, not 
  island91 malfunction. The audit criterion is circle consistency, but truth
  grouping follows the particle, not the helix: a π→µ or K→µ decay in flight, or
  a hard scatter, keeps one gtrackID across a genuine kink — one truth group,
  two circles → RESCUED or FAKE-like by the audit, with the far side of the kink
  dumped into the 13.7% "contamination." The real tracker never shows you
  those: it pre-curates (splits or drops kinked/short tracks), which is exactly
  why the real side audits cleaner (86/12/1.6) than truth-grouped sim
  (81/15/4.2). A smaller genuine reco contribution exists too — merged-cluster
  centroid pulls and edge clusters make some >3 mm outliers — but the dominant
  story is that truth groups include trajectory breaks a circle model correctly
  rejects. If you want the decomposition quantified, one pass classifying sim
  FAKE-like groups by their truth kink angle would separate "decay/scatter" from
  "bad clusters" — cheap, on request.

  5. d0 is computed identically on both sides, and don't add the offset to the 
  sim yet — first measure what the offset is. From each whole-track circle fit:
  d0 = | |C| − R |, the distance from the coordinate origin to the fitted
  circle. Sim median 0.26 cm is what perfect origin-vertices look like through
  ~700 µm clusters (fit extrapolation noise, plus a secondary tail); real median
  2.51 cm says real tracks do not point at (0,0) in this ntuple's frame. Two
  candidate causes with different correct responses: a beam-spot displacement in
  the reco frame (then the right fix is trivial and belongs with the v5.4
  vertex-spread work already in progress: place the generator vertex at the
  measured beam spot) or uncorrected coherent distortion in the stored real
  clusters (then the fix is the Q3 item — or correcting the real side — and
  moving the sim vertex would be wrong). They're distinguishable with one plot:
  signed d0 versus track azimuth — a pure translation gives a clean
  |offset|·sin(φ−φ₀) modulation whose amplitude and phase measure (x₀, y₀)
  directly; distortion gives an azimuth/drift-dependent mess (check vs median
  tbin). I'd run that diagnostic before anyone changes the generator; if it
  comes out a clean sinusoid, the offset is a two-number fix on the sim side.

❯ Do it. Why not finish it without asking me if it's essential?                 
● Fair hit — it was load-bearing for the doc and I'd already said so; offering
  instead of doing was the wrong call. Doing it now.

  The estimator, so the numbers are interpretable: for each fitted circle,
  signed d0 ≡ |C| − R plotted against the circle-center azimuth φ_c =
  atan2(b,a). A pure translation of the true vertex to (x₀,y₀) gives d0s ≈ x₀cos
  φ_c + y₀sin φ_c — a clean cosine whose amplitude and phase read off the
  offset directly, charge-independent, with curvature corrections ≤1 mm.
  Distortion instead leaves non-cosine azimuthal structure and/or drift
  dependence — tested in a second panel as the cosine-subtracted residual vs
  median tbin, with the sim as the null control on both panels.

● You're right, and point taken: the diagnostic decided which branch the doc
  could even recommend — that makes it part of finishing the doc, not an
  optional extra. It's run, and the answer surprised me:

  Verdict: the beam-spot/translation hypothesis is dead. On 3,756 real
  full-crossers, the cosine fit of signed d0 vs circle-center azimuth gives (x₀,
  y₀) = (+0.03, +0.08) cm — an 0.8 mm "offset," explaining 0% of the d0
  variance, with the sim control flat as expected. What the real data actually
  shows is (a) azimuthally isotropic per-track d0 broadening (RMS 2.99 cm vs
  sim's 1.61; medians 2.51 vs 0.26 cm), and (b) significant drift-time 
  structure: the cosine-subtracted d0 medians sit within a few mm across the
  in-time band but jump to +1.4 cm at tbin ≈ 40 and +1.8 cm at tbin ≈ 520
  (per-bin error ~0.2 cm) — the biggest biases live on out-of-time tracks. That
  pattern suggests a concrete mechanism, flagged as hypothesis in the doc: the
  real reconstruction's z-dependent r–φ distortion corrections get evaluated at
  the wrong apparent z for out-of-time tracks and mis-apply — on top of whatever
  residual static distortion/alignment imperfection produces the in-time
  high-pT 1.33 excess.

  The doc is updated and relay-ready
  (island_post/distortion_remodel_request.md): the "run this diagnostic first"
  section is replaced by the measured result, the generator branch is closed,
  the request is now two tiers — tier 1: static + module-edge distortion and
  alignment at transport (the original ask, now unambiguous); tier 2, optional:
  emulate the reco's apparent-z correction for out-of-time realism, justified by
  the drift-time structure — and the d0 acceptance meter is restated in the
  correct direction (sim should develop the isotropic broadening and, under tier
  2, the tbin structure, rather than anyone chasing a translation). New
  evidence files referenced: ms_d0diag_v53f.png/.txt, entry ms_d0diag() in
  ms_real.C.

  Had the doc gone out un-diagnosed, its leading recommendation would have
  included a two-number generator fix for an offset that doesn't exist — worth
  the ten minutes it cost.

❯ huh? you fix first or the other agent work on the doc first                   

● The other agent works from the doc — I fixed nothing in the pipeline, and
  won't. The confusion is the word "fix": what I ran first was the diagnostic (a
  measurement), not a repair. It had to precede the doc because its result
  decides which repair the doc asks for — without it, the doc would have
  requested work on a branch (generator beam-spot offset) that the measurement
  just ruled out.

  So the division of labor and the sequence from here, same as the
  truth-conflation episode in July (I found and measured it → the pipeline agent
  fixed frame_composer.C → I verified on v5.2):

  1. Done (this session, measurement side only): the evidence — circle-RMS
  data/MC 1.33, d0 broadening, and now the ms_d0diag verdict (no translation;
  isotropic + drift-time-structured → distortion-family) — all baked into
  island_post/distortion_remodel_request.md. No pipeline code was touched.
  2. You: relay the doc to the pipeline agent.
  3. Pipeline agent: implements tier 1 (static + module-edge distortion and
  alignment at the transport stage), optionally tier 2 (apparent-z
  mis-correction emulation for out-of-time tracks), and re-produces under their
  next version tag.
  4. Me, after their production lands: re-run the acceptance meters from the doc
  — ms_real / ms_real_split / ms_d0diag — expecting 1.33 → ~1.0 and the sim d0
  to develop the real broadening, while they confirm the pixel-level anchors
  didn't move (the invariance guard).

  Roles stay clean: they model, I measure — before the change to justify it,
  after the change to accept it.

● Verdict: v5.4b passes — the 1.33 is closed, and I verified it independently 
  rather than taking the ledgers on faith.

  What I checked and how:

  1. Independent reproduction. I reran ms_real_split and ms_d0diag myself
  against island91_frames_production_v54b.root under a separate tag and diffed
  against the pipeline agent's ledgers: byte-identical (their v54brev is also
  numerically identical to v54b — a re-tag, not a change). So the numbers aren't
  a wrong-input or stale-macro artifact:
    - High-pT circle RMS: real 626 µm vs sim 629 µm → data/MC = 1.00 (was 1.33).
  Primary meter closed.
    - d0 broadening: sim RMS 3.01 vs real 2.99 cm, med|d0| 2.27 vs 2.51 cm, and
  the figure's left panel now shows the sim scattering isotropically like the
  real data with a null cosine on both sides — the whole v5.3-era d0 gap has
  developed.
  2. Label invariance — exact. The v5.4b field is a positions-only overlay on
  the sealed v5.3 digi: 8,032,145 clusters with label counts 4,524,011 /
  3,207,672 / 300,462, unchanged to the last cluster from v5.3. v5.4b's 
  ntp_truth is safe for ML.
  3. The declared residuals, both visible in my rerun and honestly ledgered by 
  them:
    - Low-pT trade: med05 went 0.94 → 0.83 (sim 838 vs real 692 µm). The
  pT-blind granular scatter exposed a pre-existing low-pT over-smear in the
  response family (their decomposition: real base ~564 µm vs sim 735 before the
  field) that used to be partially compensated. Not a field-model error — but
  it's now the largest open cluster-level data/MC item, and it sits in exactly
  the window with the most tracks.
    - Tier-2 d0 structure: the drift-time median profile (+1.4 cm at tbin ≈ 40,
  +1.8 at ≈ 520) is still real-only — the sim control in panel 2 stays
  near-flat, as they declared unreachable in this model class.

  One honest science note: their probe campaign refuted my doc's mechanism. The
  real clusters turned out to be uncorrected and row-locked, the CDB static
  map's cm-scale radial bulk is incompatible with run 79507, and smooth fields
  of any kind are absorbed by a 3-parameter circle refit — the meters demanded
  short-wavelength, alignment-residual-like structure (per-layer/sector/side
  scatter at 426 µm) plus azimuthally smooth k=2,3 harmonics for d0. My request
  document pointed at the right observables but the wrong payloads; their
  measurement-first response was the correct process, and the R1 taper closing
  exactly via the per-layer radius table (v5.4a, with exact invariance) came out
  of the same audit.

  Files from my verification: ms_real_split_v54bx.{txt,png},
  ms_d0diag_v54bx.{txt,png}, ms_real_showcase_v54bx.png (my tags, their v54b
  records untouched). Memory updated. From the ML thread's side: v5.4b is now
  the preferred production — same trustworthy labels as v5.3, with track-level
  position realism the earlier versions lacked.

✻ Cooked for 4m 45s

❯ so what is med05, the high pT circle RMS fix made it worse?                   

● med05 is ledger shorthand (the pipeline agent's, which I adopted without
  expanding — my miss): the median per-track circle-fit RMS in the pT ≈ 0.5 GeV
  window; "medHi" is the same for 1.5–2.5 GeV. Same observable, two momentum
  windows.

  And yes — at face value the fix made med05 worse: real is unchanged at 692 µm
  (real data doesn't move), sim went 733 → 838 µm, so data/MC 0.94 → 0.83. But
  this was arithmetically unavoidable and scientifically an exposure, not a
  damage:

  - Unavoidable: the fix is a geometric position scatter (426 µm of
  per-layer/sector/side alignment-residual structure). Positions don't know the
  track's momentum — a geometric scatter applies to every cluster on every
  track. You cannot add it only to stiff tracks. So closing the stiff window
  (469 → 629 vs real 626) necessarily raised the 0.5 GeV window by the same
  quadrature amount: √(733² + 426²) = 848 ≈ the observed 838.
  - Exposure: the old 0.94 was two errors canceling. Decompose the real 0.5 GeV
  width: 692 µm ≈ √(564² + 426²) — a ~564 µm genuine cluster-resolution base
  plus the geometric scatter. The v5.3 sim had the opposite composition: no
  geometric scatter but an over-smeared base of 735 µm. Two different wrongs,
  nearly equal totals (733 vs 692) — the agreement was an accident of
  compensation. v5.4b put the real geometric scatter in, so the sim now reads
  735 ⊕ 426 = 838, and the actual discrepancy that existed all along stands
  exposed on its own: the sim's underlying low-pT cluster resolution is ~30% too
  wide (735 vs ~564 µm) — a response-family item
  (crossing-angle/diffusion/pad-response smearing), pre-existing since long
  before this campaign, just invisible while the compensation held.

  So the bookkeeping honestly improved even though one displayed ratio degraded:
  v5.3 had one hidden error pair masquerading as agreement; v5.4b has one
  visible, well-posed error with a ready meter — when the pipeline's response
  side brings the sim base at 0.5 GeV from 735 toward ~564 µm, med05 should land
  at √(564² + 426²) ≈ 707 ≈ real's 692, closing the last window. One caveat for
  that future fix: base and field are mildly coupled (a base change also nudges
  the stiff window, which currently sits at 1.00), so closing med05 may require
  a small re-solve of the field amplitude — the pipeline agent's ledger already
  frames it that way.

  This is the same pattern as the truth-conflation episode, worth naming because
  it keeps recurring: an aggregate that looks right can be two wrongs holding
  hands, and fixes that "worsen" a number are often the moment the real defect
  finally becomes measurable.

● Done now — it hadn't been, and it was worth it: the truth_circle battery on
  v5.4b surfaced two genuine findings the acceptance battery missed. Outputs:
  truth_circle_v54bx.{txt,png}, and the findings are appended to
  distortion_remodel_request.md as a verification addendum for the pipeline
  thread.

  What's unchanged (and had to be):

  - Truth-hit side is bit-identical to v53f — 16,626 fits, 20 µm median, R
  closure 0.9990, same species counts. Confirms v5.4b is purely an export-level
  positions overlay; nothing upstream drifted.
  - Reco-cluster RMS grew exactly as the field predicts: R-selected median 721 →
  816 µm, consistent with 721 ⊕ 426 = 838 in quadrature (the small gap is the
  R-selection reshuffling). Same story as med05, seen from the truth-matched
  side.

  What surfaced:

  1. The multi-segment fraction doubled: 1.86% → 3.69% (cluster-weighted 2.82% →
  5.03%). This is not a label regression — label counts are exactly invariant —
  it's my φ-chaining diagnostic detecting a real property of the field model:
  the hashed field is per-side, hence discontinuous at the central membrane, so
  tracks crossing z = 0 now break into two segments at the side boundary.
  Independent endplates make per-side residuals physically defensible — but the
  real side-to-side discontinuity magnitude has never been measured, and it's
  measurable: run the same chaining on real side-crossing ntp_clus_trk tracks
  (zelem is in the tree) and compare split rates. If real side-crossers don't
  split at the sim's rate, the per-side hash is too discontinuous at z ≈ 0.
  2. Curvature noise: the fraction of segments inside the ±15% R-window dropped 
  95.0% → 87.4%, with the median segment R_fit shifting 117.7 → 118.5 cm. The
  k=2,3 harmonics vary along a track's φ-span, so they are not a pure per-track
  translation ("a translated circle is a circle" only holds for constant shifts)
  — they inject few-percent-scale curvature noise, i.e., an effective
  pT-resolution degradation. This mode was not among the acceptance meters.
  Whether real data carries the same curvature noise is checkable without truth:
  compare per-track half-arc curvature consistency (R_inner vs R_outer from the
  split fits) between real and sim — proposed in the addendum as an additional
  meter before the field model gets sealed.

  Neither finding blocks v5.4b for label-level (ML) use — labels are exact, and
  the RMS behavior is understood. Both are shape questions about the field
  model: is the CM discontinuity real-sized, and is the injected curvature noise
  real-sized? They're now in the doc with concrete real-data checks attached,
  for the pipeline agent to judge when you relay.

❯ Question 1 (CM discontinuity): the other thread's instinct was right, with a  
  measured size. Real side-crossers show a genuine membrane discontinuity —     
  med|J| = 0.480 cm against a 0.304 fit-noise floor (≈0.37 cm genuine) — so     
  per-side residuals are physically correct, but v5.4b's fully independent      
  per-side phases overshoot it ~2.5× (0.985 cm) and cost half the side-crossing 
  sample through the rms gate. The v5.4c fix keeps the physics and fixes the    
  size: main harmonics side-shared (continuous at the membrane), plus a small   
  independent per-side harmonic (SCM = 0.26 cm) sized to land on real's 0.48,   
  with total azimuthal power preserved so the d0 meters shouldn't move.         
                                                                                
  Question 2 (curvature noise): resolved in v5.4b's favor — no change. Real's   
  own half-arc curvature inconsistency (dk-width 0.402 at 0.5 GeV) is wider     
  than what the harmonics inject (0.273, over a 0.241 floor). Their R-window    
  drop detected a real mode of the model, but the real detector carries more of 
  it, not less — the field is conservative on this axis. v5.4c is finalized     
  and handed off. Closing state:

  Two things worth appreciating in how this closed. First, both of my v5.4b
  shape questions were answered the right way — by measurement, in both 
  directions: the membrane discontinuity turned out to be genuinely physical
  (real side-crossers jump 0.48 cm above a 0.30 fit floor — their per-side

  undersized on that axis, not oversized). Second, their restraint at the end is
  the correct statistics call: with the real membrane sample at n=48, trimming
  SCM below 0.26 cm would be fitting noise.

● Updating the thread-state memory with the campaign results:

● Campaign complete — all four items done on v5.4c, and the comprehensive MS
  conclusion is written to island_post/ms_conclusion_v54c.md.

  The verdict (item 4, one paragraph): MS needs no explicit treatment anywhere
  in the chain, sim or data. Truth trajectory is a circle to 20 µm (5×10⁻⁴ of
  sagitta); truth MS is 1.37 mrad at 0.5 GeV (1/p-scaling verified);
  cluster-level tangent noise is 18–31 mrad and pT-flat on both sides —
  resolution, ~20× above MS. The real data shows it directly: real σ(Δψ) does
  not grow at low pT. The effect worth modeling is distortion (d0 ~2.5 cm,
  membrane jump 0.48 cm), which is what v5.4 addresses.

  Item 1 — ntp_clus_trk level (v54cx): σ(Δψ) real/sim 25.7/27.8 mrad at 0.5 GeV,
  31.2/30.2 stiff; circle RMS 692/842 µm (0.82) and 626/635 µm (0.99 — the old
  +33% real excess is confirmed gone on v5.4c); d0 2.51/2.37 cm. Figure QA
  caught a real defect: the split figure's right panel still asserted the
  v53f-era "+33% real excess" while its own table read 0.99 — the conclusion
  lines are now computed from the measured ratios, and the truth-MS citation
  ("1.07/0.31") is now correctly attributed to the r0scan r0=49 row rather than
  a nonexistent v54cx ms_split run.

  Item 2 — symmetric cluster level (new mt_cluscmp): the same exhaustive finder
  grouped both full cluster trees, no tracker or truth involved. Real complete
  events: 184 circles/ev; sim: 385/ev → rate 0.48 at only 0.88× the cluster 
  occupancy — sim frames are ~2× more track-organized per cluster. That's a new
  open finding on the content/organization axis (relay-worthy to the pipeline
  session; I've kept it in my own files per the scope rule). RMS 658/815 µm
  (0.81, same family as the known low-pT over-smear); in-time fraction 0.34 vs
  0.33 — the time structure of found circles matches well.

  Item 3 — pre-clustering (new mt_g4scan + mt_pixscan): On sim truth hits the
  finder recovers 96% of the 14.06 findable circles/collision; the raw rate
  (34/coll) is inflated by second arcs of already-found particles (42%) and
  mixed chains (17%) — the caveat that applies to any raw dense-point rate.
  Bridge closes: 333 findable/frame ÷ 14.06/collision = 23.7 collisions/frame.
  On real raw pixels (25 complete events): 293 track-class circles/event 
  straight from pixels, RMS 805 µm (pad centers — cluster 658 µm plus
  quantization), in-time 0.36. One honest limitation stated in the doc: sim
  pixel trees carry no v5.4 field (the overlay lives at island91 export), so the
  sim pre-clustering anchor is the truth-hit scan.

  Two process notes: an invocation slip on my side re-ran the default
  missed_tracks() entry and regenerated missed_tracks_v53f.{txt,png} in place —
  the output is number-identical to the era record (77.7/75.2/127.28/67.2), so
  no harm, and it doubles as the regression proof that the hunt(cohband, 
  cohslope) generalization preserves behavior. All new figures (ms_cluscmp_v54c,
  ms_pixscan_v54c, regenerated ms_real_split_v54cx + showcase) are QA'd; no
  stray processes left.

  Open items on the ledgers: the 0.82 low-pT over-smear (response family,
  pre-existing) and the new 0.48 organization rate — both belong to the
  pipeline's lane if you choose to relay them.
